# Unit 29 linguistic and owned-edition mapping packet

This packet prepares review. It records an unsigned structural mapping proposal. It does not record a qualified review, an approved edition alignment, or an install decision.

The owned-edition mapping worksheet separates observed edition facts, the proposed lesson and printed page range, unresolved edition questions, and matters that still require qualified linguistic review. ISBN, owned-copy verification, variance, reviewer identity, qualification, date, and approval stay blank. The disposition remains PENDING_CURRICULUM_REVIEWER.

Unit 29 remains in development. Its readiness is content-complete and review-pending. The Lesson 29 relationship is a proposed, unsigned mapping. It is not an approved edition alignment.

kind=TEACH
title=Distinguish active past and present descriptions
body=An active participle describes an entity performing the action. The retained чита́вшая is the past reading form; чита́ющая is the present reading form. The labels in the past and in the present distinguish these supplied forms. They do not identify a passive entity being read. An English description of reading can express the action without itself specifying this past/present contrast.

kind=TEACH
title=Read the question as well as the form
body=For чита́ющая, select in the present when asked for its time label. For чита́вшая, select in the past for its own time label. Classify the displayed form itself. Selecting the other time label would test following a reversal instruction rather than interpreting the participle. The gloss that read gives an action meaning rather than the requested time classification.

kind=TEACH
title=Recognize the separate living example
body=The retained живу́щие means living. In the supplied example, people perform the living action; this is an active present participle. Its time classification is in the present, while living is its lexical meaning. This is recognition of the admitted example, not a general algorithm for generating participles of unfamiliar verbs. Interpreting an isolated form does not establish the complete tense or actor of a sentence that has not been supplied.

- Content hash: bcc86875c18107b45e29dd1cdbf52d07a544603674fb201a6574317c79d88842

kind=REMEDIATE
title=Review the complete retained-example contrast
body=An active participle describes an entity performing the action. The retained чита́вшая is the past reading form; чита́ющая is the present reading form. The labels in the past and in the present distinguish these supplied forms. They do not identify a passive entity being read. An English description of reading can express the action without itself specifying this past/present contrast.

For чита́ющая, select in the present when asked for its time label. For чита́вшая, select in the past for its own time label. Classify the displayed form itself. Selecting the other time label would test following a reversal instruction rather than interpreting the participle. The gloss that read gives an action meaning rather than the requested time classification.

The retained живу́щие means living. In the supplied example, people perform the living action; this is an active present participle. Its time classification is in the present, while living is its lexical meaning. This is recognition of the admitted example, not a general algorithm for generating participles of unfamiliar verbs. Interpreting an isolated form does not establish the complete tense or actor of a sentence that has not been supplied.

- Content hash: b69fd06c857d5092fa797b310c4f177dcf7d80189f64a0269aef496fdc2a0db0

kind=TEACH
title=Recognize the long and short forms
body=The retained но́вый and нов both mean new. The first is the long form and the second is the masculine singular short form. The grammatical check asks which form is shown, rather than its English gloss. Long adjectives have case, gender and number inflection; short adjectives vary for gender and number, not case. The retained examples are recognition targets, not a universal rule for producing short forms by removing endings.
dominantAction=Continue
references=ref-u2630-attribution

unavailableReason=

- Sessions: u29-s-short-adjective

- Current review status: NO_SEPARATE_REVIEW_FIELD
- Content hash: 4b846c8d590cde526d3bbcd70412ed0aeae1d0591cc713c57d18d25a6b09b2c0

kind=TEACH
title=Distinguish a predicate from a noun description
body=In the taught modern use, short adjectives occur only in the nominative and are used predicatively to describe their subject. A long adjective often describes a noun attributively and can also be a predicate. The choice depends on the sentence, not a universal rule about one fixed word position. The isolated pair does not provide a complete context for selecting every long-versus-short form; broader agreement and sentence-level application remain pending.

title=A short-looking word need not be a short adjective
body=The retained беж means beige and is an invariant colour form, unchanged for case, gender and number. Its short appearance does not make it the short counterpart of но́вый. Keep lexical meaning, inflection and adjective function separate. The correct glosses remain in Vocabulary Lab as word-meaning practice; they do not satisfy the independent adjective grammar checks.

title=Review the complete retained-example contrast
body=The retained но́вый and нов both mean new. The first is the long form and the second is the masculine singular short form. The grammatical check asks which form is shown, rather than its English gloss. Long adjectives have case, gender and number inflection; short adjectives vary for gender and number, not case. The retained examples are recognition targets, not a universal rule for producing short forms by removing endings.

In the taught modern use, short adjectives occur only in the nominative and are used predicatively to describe their subject. A long adjective often describes a noun attributively and can also be a predicate. The choice depends on the sentence, not a universal rule about one fixed word position. The isolated pair does not provide a complete context for selecting every long-versus-short form; broader agreement and sentence-level application remain pending.

The retained беж means beige and is an invariant colour form, unchanged for case, gender and number. Its short appearance does not make it the short counterpart of но́вый. Keep lexical meaning, inflection and adjective function separate. The correct glosses remain in Vocabulary Lab as word-meaning practice; they do not satisfy the independent adjective grammar checks.
dominantAction=Continue
references=
items=
examples=ex-u29-short-adjective-0,ex-u29-short-adjective-1,ex-u29-short-adjective-2

kind=TEACH
title=Compare two ways of expressing the same sequence
body=The supplied Пообедав, я пошёл гулять means Having eaten, I went for a walk. The opening verbal adverb presents eating before the walking action. The supplied Я пообедал и пошёл гулять gives the sequence with finite verbs joined together. Both describe the same person eating and then going for a walk; they differ in expression.

kind=TEACH
title=Separate register labels from event meaning
body=In this retained source comparison the verbal-adverb version is labelled bookish speech and the finite-verb version colloquial language. Those labels describe the contrasted register; Having eaten, I went for a walk is the event meaning rather than a register label. A sentence appearing on paper is not automatically bookish, and this pair does not license labelling every verbal adverb in every context as bookish.

- Content hash: 6808114c5997c3c65497b2e8e8210f524320ea07cb05364b1ab6d8293d374f9a

kind=TEACH
title=Contrast the adverbial opening with finite coordination
body=In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту., finishing the letter is expressed by the opening verbal adverb. In Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту., the actions are expressed by coordinated finite verbs. The adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination the ordinary option in this admitted pair. Both describe finishing before going to the post office; the register choice does not reverse those events.

title=Keep the register claim inside this particular pair
body=The supplied Написанное письмо лежит на столе. contains a passive description and is outside this adverbial-versus-finite pair. Mere appearance in writing also does not establish this bookish classification. A verbal adverb in a different sentence needs its own contextual evidence: this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish. Recognizing the opening word is different from interpreting the whole sentence or judging an unfamiliar style.

kind=TEACH
title=Follow the actor and the order of actions
body=In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту и отправила его., Anna finishes the letter before going to the post office and sending it. Anna performs the actions; the letter does not travel alone and the post office does not write it. The finishing phrase belongs before the departure, not after the sending. Read the entire supplied passage when a check shows only a fragment.
dominantAction=Continue

- Objectives: obj-u29-reading, obj-u29-book

- Content hash: 18821a6665553a24f269ed2b4c38fcf9948ce809dab16668b70047a5d94051ca

kind=TEACH
title=Resolve the final action without substituting a register answer
body=The supplied отправила его means she sent it, with the letter as the object in this passage. The passage places the sending after Anna goes to the post office. It does not say that she stayed at the table, finished at the market, left the letter at home or received a letter at home. Those alternatives replace a stated event or location. Bookish register classification is a separate objective and cannot answer who acted, where she went or what she did with the letter.

kind=REMEDIATE
title=Review the complete retained-example contrast
body=In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту и отправила его., Anna finishes the letter before going to the post office and sending it. Anna performs the actions; the letter does not travel alone and the post office does not write it. The finishing phrase belongs before the departure, not after the sending. Read the entire supplied passage when a check shows only a fragment.

The supplied отправила его means she sent it, with the letter as the object in this passage. The passage places the sending after Anna goes to the post office. It does not say that she stayed at the table, finished at the market, left the letter at home or received a letter at home. Those alternatives replace a stated event or location. Bookish register classification is a separate objective and cannot answer who acted, where she went or what she did with the letter.
dominantAction=Continue
references=ref-u2630-attribution

title=Units 26–30 source notice
summary=Proposed Units 26–30 adapt admitted CC BY-SA 4.0 spans. Unsupported topics stay in the copy you own. Bounded postal, stress, and dictionary tasks are review-pending.
detail=Wikipedia article text is frozen by oldid under CC BY-SA 4.0 via Wikipedia:Copyrights oldid 1369466282. The Wikibooks address note is frozen by oldid under the separate CC BY-SA 4.0 route recorded on Wikibooks:Copyrights oldid 4622060. The packaged CC BY-SA 4.0 legal code is cc-by-sa-4.0.txt. The adapted selection and arrangement are share-alike. Application code, user data, user recordings, and separately licensed audio are outside that statement. No audio is included. Linked media, Phonos, and pronunciation files are not admitted. Anecdotal postal advice is not shipped. Russian Wikipedia morphology oldid 148939075 is admitted only for the abbreviation declension contrast and the command-versus-supposition particle contrast, under the Russian Wikipedia copyrights oldid 154562152. Russian Wikipedia indefinite-pronoun page oldid 149963314 is admitted for the series meanings under that same Russian Wikipedia copyrights route. Russian Wikipedia participle page oldid 153078996 is admitted for the participial-phrase punctuation rule, including its exceptions, under that same route. Complete postal mastery, general participle-stress rules, universal register judgments, and broad dictionary proficiency are not supplied by these bounded tasks. ReadyRussian letter conventions cover structure, the Russian and English organizational difference, and the stranger address limit. The abbreviation contrast and the particle contrast do not activate an authority variance. Review is pending. Bounded postal practice, past-passive stress, and a two-entry dictionary task are implemented and review-pending. They are not complete postal mastery, a general stress rule, or broad dictionary proficiency. Present-passive exploration does not gate Unit 27.

originalSourceText=Retained Wikipedia Russian grammar oldid1369617627, catalog/sources/units-26-30/wikipedia/russian-grammar-1369617627.txt, CC BY-SA 4.0 under retained FREEZE rights attestation: complete Adjectives introduction, long/short declension table and qualifications before Comparison of adjectives. Immutable admitted short-form capability sourceHash0b5b1bad657a8c901ba9f471ff7adff3a945be6f281aa760f58c59fd0f8c78e8 identifies the invariant beige and khaki forms, the supplied new long/short pair, and short forms in nominative/predicative use. Only the already admitted long new, short new and invariant beige forms are classified. Short adjectives vary for gender/number, not case; some adjectives lack short forms, and spelling/stress cannot be generated by a universal truncation rule. Long forms can also be predicates: do not infer an exceptionless word-position rule. Vocabulary glosses remain available as optional word-meaning practice and do not satisfy the grammar gate. Broader agreement and sentence-level predicate/attribute application remain pending. Independently authored English on 2026-10-13 within content-audit-2026-10-08; no new Russian forms, copied primary prose or private Penguin text; qualified semantic audit pending.
Distinguish admitted long, short and invariant adjective forms

originalSourceText=Retained Wikipedia Russian grammar oldid1369617627, catalog/sources/units-26-30/wikipedia/russian-grammar-1369617627.txt, CC BY-SA 4.0 under retained FREEZE rights attestation: complete Adjectives introduction, long/short declension table and qualifications before Comparison of adjectives. Immutable admitted short-form capability sourceHash0b5b1bad657a8c901ba9f471ff7adff3a945be6f281aa760f58c59fd0f8c78e8 identifies the invariant beige and khaki forms, the supplied new long/short pair, and short forms in nominative/predicative use. Only the already admitted long new, short new and invariant beige forms are classified. Short adjectives vary for gender/number, not case; some adjectives lack short forms, and spelling/stress cannot be generated by a universal truncation rule. Long forms can also be predicates: do not infer an exceptionless word-position rule. Vocabulary glosses remain available as optional word-meaning practice and do not satisfy the grammar gate. Independently authored English on 2026-10-13 within content-audit-2026-10-08; no new Russian forms, copied primary prose or private Penguin text; qualified semantic audit pending.
The retained но́вый and нов both mean new. The first is the long form and the second is the masculine singular short form. The grammatical check asks which form is shown, rather than its English gloss. Long adjectives have case, gender and number inflection; short adjectives vary for gender and number, not case. The retained examples are recognition targets, not a universal rule for producing short forms by removing endings.

In the taught modern use, short adjectives occur only in the nominative and are used predicatively to describe their subject. A long adjective often describes a noun attributively and can also be a predicate. The choice depends on the sentence, not a universal rule about one fixed word position. The isolated pair does not provide a complete context for selecting every long-versus-short form; broader agreement and sentence-level application remain pending.

The retained беж means beige and is an invariant colour form, unchanged for case, gender and number. Its short appearance does not make it the short counterpart of но́вый. Keep lexical meaning, inflection and adjective function separate. The correct glosses remain in Vocabulary Lab as word-meaning practice; they do not satisfy the independent adjective grammar checks.

Immutable admitted short-form capability sourceHash0b5b1bad657a8c901ba9f471ff7adff3a945be6f281aa760f58c59fd0f8c78e8 identifies the invariant beige and khaki forms, the supplied new long/short pair, and short forms in nominative/predicative use. Only the already admitted long new, short new and invariant beige forms are classified. Short adjectives vary for gender/number, not case; some adjectives lack short forms, and spelling/stress cannot be generated by a universal truncation rule. Long forms can also be predicates: do not infer an exceptionless word-position rule. Vocabulary glosses remain available as optional word-meaning practice and do not satisfy the grammar gate. Broader agreement and sentence-level predicate/attribute application remain pending. Independently authored English on 2026-10-13 within content-audit-2026-10-08; no new Russian forms, copied primary prose or private Penguin text; qualified semantic audit pending.
The retained беж means beige and is an invariant colour form, unchanged for case, gender and number. Its short appearance does not make it the short counterpart of но́вый. Keep lexical meaning, inflection and adjective function separate. The correct glosses remain in Vocabulary Lab as word-meaning practice; they do not satisfy the independent adjective grammar checks.

An active participle describes an entity performing the action. The retained чита́вшая is the past reading form; чита́ющая is the present reading form. The labels in the past and in the present distinguish these supplied forms. They do not identify a passive entity being read. An English description of reading can express the action without itself specifying this past/present contrast.

An active participle describes an entity performing the action. The retained чита́вшая is the past reading form; чита́ющая is the present reading form. The labels in the past and in the present distinguish these supplied forms. They do not identify a passive entity being read. An English description of reading can express the action without itself specifying this past/present contrast.

For чита́ющая, select in the present when asked for its time label. For чита́вшая, select in the past for its own time label. One retained contrast check instead asks for the other time: there the answer is in the present. That reversed question does not turn the displayed past form into a present form. The gloss that read names an action; it is not the requested contrasting time label.

The retained живу́щие has the meaning living and illustrates an active description. This is recognition of the admitted example, not a general algorithm for generating participles of unfamiliar verbs. Interpreting an isolated form does not establish the complete tense or actor of a sentence that has not been supplied.

sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08

originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту и отправила его., Anna finishes the letter before going to the post office and sending it. Anna performs the actions; the letter does not travel alone and the post office does not write it. The finishing phrase belongs before the departure, not after the sending. Read the entire supplied passage when a check shows only a fragment.

originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту и отправила его., Anna finishes the letter before going to the post office and sending it. Anna performs the actions; the letter does not travel alone and the post office does not write it. The finishing phrase belongs before the departure, not after the sending. Read the entire supplied passage when a check shows only a fragment.

The supplied отправила его means she sent it, with the letter as the object in this passage. The passage places the sending after Anna goes to the post office. It does not say that she stayed at the table, finished at the market, left the letter at home or received a letter at home. Those alternatives replace a stated event or location. Bookish register classification is a separate objective and cannot answer who acted, where she went or what she did with the letter.

It does not say that she stayed at the table, finished at the market, left the letter at home or received a letter at home. Those alternatives replace a stated event or location. Bookish register classification is a separate objective and cannot answer who acted, where she went or what she did with the letter.

originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту., finishing the letter is expressed by the opening verbal adverb. In Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту., the actions are expressed by coordinated finite verbs. The adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination the ordinary option in this admitted pair. Both describe finishing before going to the post office; the register choice does not reverse those events.
shippedText=In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту., finishing the letter is expressed by the opening verbal adverb. In Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту., the actions are expressed by coordinated finite verbs. The adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination the ordinary option in this admitted pair. Both describe finishing before going to the post office; the register choice does not reverse those events.

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u29-register:u29-s-gap-register-3
originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
The supplied Написанное письмо лежит на столе. contains a passive description and is outside this adverbial-versus-finite pair. Mere appearance in writing also does not establish this bookish classification. A verbal adverb in a different sentence needs its own contextual evidence: this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish. Recognizing the opening word is different from interpreting the whole sentence or judging an unfamiliar style.
shippedText=The supplied Написанное письмо лежит на столе. contains a passive description and is outside this adverbial-versus-finite pair. Mere appearance in writing also does not establish this bookish classification. A verbal adverb in a different sentence needs its own contextual evidence: this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish. Recognizing the opening word is different from interpreting the whole sentence or judging an unfamiliar style.

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u29-register:u29-s-gap-register-7
originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту., finishing the letter is expressed by the opening verbal adverb. In Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту., the actions are expressed by coordinated finite verbs. The adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination the ordinary option in this admitted pair. Both describe finishing before going to the post office; the register choice does not reverse those events.

The supplied Написанное письмо лежит на столе. contains a passive description and is outside this adverbial-versus-finite pair. Mere appearance in writing also does not establish this bookish classification. A verbal adverb in a different sentence needs its own contextual evidence: this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish. Recognizing the opening word is different from interpreting the whole sentence or judging an unfamiliar style.
shippedText=In Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту., finishing the letter is expressed by the opening verbal adverb. In Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту., the actions are expressed by coordinated finite verbs. The adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination the ordinary option in this admitted pair. Both describe finishing before going to the post office; the register choice does not reverse those events.

The supplied Написанное письмо лежит на столе. contains a passive description and is outside this adverbial-versus-finite pair. Mere appearance in writing also does not establish this bookish classification. A verbal adverb in a different sentence needs its own contextual evidence: this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish. Recognizing the opening word is different from interpreting the whole sentence or judging an unfamiliar style.

In Программа, написанная в спешке, выполнила недопустимую операцию. The phrase describing the program follows its noun and is set off by commas on both sides. originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
The retained rule identifies exceptions when the described entity is a personal pronoun or the participial phrase expresses a cause. The displayed личным местоимением names the personal-pronoun case; кроме тех случаев indicates the exception boundary, not the default no-comma treatment. Recognize that exception label here; this small set does not demonstrate punctuation of every unfamiliar participial sentence.

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u29-punctuation:u29-s-punctuation-9
originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
In Программа, написанная в спешке, выполнила недопустимую операцию. The phrase describing the program follows its noun and is set off by commas on both sides. The retained label выделяется запятыми с двух сторон names that treatment. A single comma before the phrase would omit its closing boundary in this supplied sentence.

In Написанная в спешке программа выполнила недопустимую операцию. The same descriptive phrase precedes the noun. The supplied example has no separating commas: запятые не ставятся is its label. Phrase position helps distinguish these two examples; this does not mean commas are never used before a described noun.

The retained rule identifies exceptions when the described entity is a personal pronoun or the participial phrase expresses a cause. The displayed личным местоимением names the personal-pronoun case; кроме тех случаев indicates the exception boundary, not the default no-comma treatment. Recognize that exception label here; this small set does not demonstrate punctuation of every unfamiliar participial sentence.
shippedText=In Программа, написанная в спешке, выполнила недопустимую операцию. The phrase describing the program follows its noun and is set off by commas on both sides. The retained label выделяется запятыми с двух сторон names that treatment. A single comma before the phrase would omit its closing boundary in this supplied sentence.

In Написанная в спешке программа выполнила недопустимую операцию. The same descriptive phrase precedes the noun. The supplied example has no separating commas: запятые не ставятся is its label. Phrase position helps distinguish these two examples; this does not mean commas are never used before a described noun.

The retained rule identifies exceptions when the described entity is a personal pronoun or the participial phrase expresses a cause. The displayed личным местоимением names the personal-pronoun case; кроме тех случаев indicates the exception boundary, not the default no-comma treatment. Recognize that exception label here; this small set does not demonstrate punctuation of every unfamiliar participial sentence.

originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
The retained но́вый and нов both mean new. The first is the long form and the second is the short form. Their shared meaning distinguishes them from the colour meanings beige and khaki-colored. Choosing the English gloss alone does not demonstrate selecting the appropriate adjective form in a complete sentence.

- Content hash: 955857c74251cc1425f2da92981da5473cc84876f54b879b26996e94431d2faf

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u29-short-adjective:u29-s-short-adjective-3
originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
A short adjective often supplies the predicate describing its subject; a long adjective often describes a noun attributively. The choice depends on the sentence, not a universal rule about one fixed word position. The retained isolated pair does not provide a complete context for deciding every long-versus-short choice. Do not treat the common predicate/attribute contrast as an exceptionless rule.
shippedText=A short adjective often supplies the predicate describing its subject; a long adjective often describes a noun attributively. The choice depends on the sentence, not a universal rule about one fixed word position. The retained isolated pair does not provide a complete context for deciding every long-versus-short choice. Do not treat the common predicate/attribute contrast as an exceptionless rule.

recordType=label
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:content-audit-research:2026-10-08
sourceRevision=content-audit-2026-10-08
sourceRecordId=ca-coverage:obj-u29-short-adjective:u29-s-short-adjective-9
originalSourceText=Retained Russian grammar revision 1369617627, retained participle punctuation revision 153078996, and existing hash-bound Unit29 research register/reading spans. Independently authored English instruction, 2026-10-12. No private book expression, new Russian or universal productive rule. Pending final linguistic audit.
The retained но́вый and нов both mean new. The first is the long form and the second is the short form. Their shared meaning distinguishes them from the colour meanings beige and khaki-colored. Choosing the English gloss alone does not demonstrate selecting the appropriate adjective form in a complete sentence.

A short adjective often supplies the predicate describing its subject; a long adjective often describes a noun attributively. The choice depends on the sentence, not a universal rule about one fixed word position. The retained isolated pair does not provide a complete context for deciding every long-versus-short choice. Do not treat the common predicate/attribute contrast as an exceptionless rule.

The retained беж means beige and is an invariant colour form. Its short appearance does not make it the short counterpart of но́вый. Keep meaning, inflection and adjective function separate. This set checks supplied gloss recognition and explains the contrast; it does not demonstrate productive adjective agreement or sentence-level form selection.

The choice depends on the sentence, not a universal rule about one fixed word position. The retained isolated pair does not provide a complete context for deciding every long-versus-short choice. Do not treat the common predicate/attribute contrast as an exceptionless rule.

The retained беж means beige and is an invariant colour form. Its short appearance does not make it the short counterpart of но́вый. Keep meaning, inflection and adjective function separate. originalSourceText=Retained Wikipedia Russian grammar oldid1369617627, catalog/sources/units-26-30/wikipedia/russian-grammar-1369617627.txt, CC BY-SA 4.0 under retained FREEZE rights attestation: complete Adjectival participle introduction and Active present participle / Active past participle sections. The already admitted living form is the example immediately under Active present participle; the separate reading examples distinguish past and present. This bounded classification identifies participial tense, not the time of an unseen sentence or a productive formation algorithm. Original reversal followed its literal prompt; its defect was awarding time-comprehension credit for choosing the complementary label, not mistranslating a form. Replace it with the distinct already admitted living form. Independently authored English on 2026-10-13 within content-audit-2026-10-08; no new Russian forms, copied primary prose or private Penguin text; qualified semantic audit pending.
Context explanation:
живу́щие means living and is the active present participle in the supplied example: the people perform the living action. Select in the present for this form itself. The past label describes a different participial tense; living gives the lexical meaning rather than the requested time classification. This identifies the admitted form, not the tense of an unseen sentence.

An active participle describes an entity performing the action. The retained чита́вшая is the past reading form; чита́ющая is the present reading form. The labels in the past and in the present distinguish these supplied forms. They do not identify a passive entity being read. An English description of reading can express the action without itself specifying this past/present contrast.

For чита́ющая, select in the present when asked for its time label. For чита́вшая, select in the past for its own time label. Classify the displayed form itself. Selecting the other time label would test following a reversal instruction rather than interpreting the participle. The gloss that read gives an action meaning rather than the requested time classification.

The retained живу́щие means living. In the supplied example, people perform the living action; this is an active present participle. Its time classification is in the present, while living is its lexical meaning. This is recognition of the admitted example, not a general algorithm for generating participles of unfamiliar verbs. Interpreting an isolated form does not establish the complete tense or actor of a sentence that has not been supplied.

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:The active past participle is used in order to indicate actions that happened in
originalSourceText=The active past participle is used in order to indicate actions that happened in the past:
:Де́вушка, '''чита́вшая''' тут кни́гу, забы́ла свой телефо́н – The girl '''that read''' this book here forgot her phone (the girl read the book '''in the past''').

Compare:
:Де́вушка, '''чита́ющая''' тут кни́гу, – моя́ сестра́ – The girl '''reading''' this book here is my sister (she is reading the book now, '''in the present''').
shippedText=in the past
transformation=selection

originalSourceText=In standard Russian, adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish speech; in colloquial language they are usually replaced with single adjectival participles or constructions with verbs: Пообедав, я пошёл гулять ("Having eaten, I went for a walk") → Я пообедал и пошёл гулять ("I ate and went for a walk").

recordType=meaning
sourceUrl=https://ru.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Причастие_(лингвистика)&oldid=153078996
sourceRevision=153078996
sourceRecordId=prichastie:В русском языке причастный оборот часто выделяется [[Запятая|запятыми]]. Если пр
originalSourceText=В русском языке причастный оборот часто выделяется [[Запятая|запятыми]]. Если причастный оборот стоит после определяемого слова, он выделяется запятыми с двух сторон. Когда причастный оборот стоит перед определяемым словом, запятые не ставятся, кроме тех случаев, когда определяемое слово выражено личным местоимением или причастный оборот обозначает причину. Если после причастного оборота конец предложения, то запятая ставится только перед причастным оборотом.

'''Примеры:'''
* ''Программа, написанная в спешке, выполнила недопустимую операцию.''
* ''Написанная в спешке программа выполнила недопустимую операцию.
shippedText=выделяется запятыми с двух сторон
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/
attribution=Участники русской Википедии, CC BY-SA 4.0, via Википедия:Авторские права oldid 154562152. Page history for oldid 153078996 identifies the contributors.
shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.
sourceHash=821d77bb38feb19b79078e8af3f507f2fcaa4d7e6735f4c1d10a2475a73cbc9a

recordType=meaning

sourceRevision=153078996
sourceRecordId=prichastie:В русском языке причастный оборот часто выделяется [[Запятая|запятыми]]. Если пр
originalSourceText=В русском языке причастный оборот часто выделяется [[Запятая|запятыми]]. Если причастный оборот стоит после определяемого слова, он выделяется запятыми с двух сторон. Когда причастный оборот стоит перед определяемым словом, запятые не ставятся, кроме тех случаев, когда определяемое слово выражено личным местоимением или причастный оборот обозначает причину. Если после причастного оборота конец предложения, то запятая ставится только перед причастным оборотом.

'''Примеры:'''
* ''Программа, написанная в спешке, выполнила недопустимую операцию.''
* ''Написанная в спешке программа выполнила недопустимую операцию.
shippedText=кроме тех случаев
transformation=selection

originalSourceText=such as {{wikt-lang|ru|беж}} ('beige', non-adapted form of {{wikt-lang|ru|бе́жевый}}) or {{lang|ru|ха́ки}} ('khaki-colored'), most adjectives follow one of a small number of regular declension patterns (except for some that complicate the {{wikt-lang|en|short form}}). In modern Russian, the short form appears only in the nominative and is used when the adjective is in a [[predicate (grammar)|predicative]] role: {{lang|ru|нов, нова́, но́во, новы́}} are short forms of {{lang|ru|но́вый}} ('new').

recordType=original-synthesis
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:original-research-synthesis:phase3-2026-09-30
sourceRevision=phase3-2026-09-30
sourceRecordId=phase3:bookish-reading
originalSourceText=Read the passage for the actions, not for whether the style is bookish.
Register classification is a different objective and does not answer these readings.
Return to what Anna finished, where she went, and what she sent.
Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту и отправила его.
she went to the post office after finishing
отправила его
she sent the letter from the post office
Which English reading matches the person who acts in this passage?
Анна
Anna is the person who did the actions
Admitted reading: Anna is the person who did the actions
the letter went to the post office alone
the post office wrote the letter
Rejected reading: the letter went to the post office alone
Rejected reading: the post office wrote the letter
Which English reading matches what happened before Anna left?
Закончив письмо
she finished the letter before leaving
Admitted reading: she finished the letter before leaving
she sent the letter before writing it
she left before the letter existed
Rejected reading: she sent the letter before writing it
Rejected reading: she left before the letter existed
Which English reading matches where Anna went after finishing?
пошла на почту
Admitted reading: she went to the post office after finishing
she stayed at the table after finishing
she finished the letter at the market
Rejected reading: she stayed at the table after finishing
Rejected reading: she finished the letter at the market
Which English reading matches what Anna did with the letter at the end?
Admitted reading: she sent the letter from the post office
she left the letter at home
she received a letter at home
Rejected reading: she left the letter at home
Rejected reading: she received a letter at home
Закончив письмо,
Анна пошла на почту и отправила его.
Анна пошла на почту и отправила его

recordType=original-synthesis
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:original-research-synthesis:phase3-2026-09-30
sourceRevision=phase3-2026-09-30
sourceRecordId=phase3:bookish-register
originalSourceText=In this admitted pair, the adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination is the ordinary option.
A passive participle, text that is merely on paper, or a different sentence does not extend this pair.
This pair is not a universal stylistic rule.
Return to the adverbial opening versus the finite coordination in this pair.
Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту.
the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту.
the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair
Which English description identifies the opening word of the bookish sentence in this pair?
Закончив
the adverbial word opens the bookish sentence
Admitted reading: the adverbial word opens the bookish sentence
a passive participle is outside this bookish pair
Rejected reading: the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair
Rejected reading: a passive participle is outside this bookish pair
Which English description identifies the bookish option in this admitted pair?
Admitted reading: the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Rejected reading: appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Which English description identifies the ordinary option in this admitted pair?
Admitted reading: the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair
Rejected reading: the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
Which English description identifies a passive participle that is outside this admitted pair?
Написанное письмо лежит на столе.
Admitted reading: a passive participle is outside this bookish pair
Which English description identifies text that is merely on paper?
a sentence that is only on paper
Admitted reading: appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Which English description states the limit of this admitted pair?
an adverbial in a different sentence
this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish
Admitted reading: this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish
every adverbial participle is bookish in every sentence
Rejected reading: every adverbial participle is bookish in every sentence
Закончив письмо,
Закончив письмо
Анна пошла на почту.
Анна пошла на почту
Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту
Написанное письмо лежит на столе
shippedText=In this admitted pair, the adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination is the ordinary option.

recordType=original-synthesis
sourceUrl=urn:russian-companion:original-research-synthesis:phase3-2026-09-30
sourceRevision=phase3-2026-09-30
sourceRecordId=phase3:bookish-register
originalSourceText=In this admitted pair, the adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination is the ordinary option.
A passive participle, text that is merely on paper, or a different sentence does not extend this pair.
This pair is not a universal stylistic rule.
Return to the adverbial opening versus the finite coordination in this pair.
Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту.
the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту.
the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair
Which English description identifies the opening word of the bookish sentence in this pair?
Закончив
the adverbial word opens the bookish sentence
Admitted reading: the adverbial word opens the bookish sentence
a passive participle is outside this bookish pair
Rejected reading: the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair
Rejected reading: a passive participle is outside this bookish pair
Which English description identifies the bookish option in this admitted pair?
Admitted reading: the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Rejected reading: appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Which English description identifies the ordinary option in this admitted pair?
Admitted reading: the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair
Rejected reading: the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
Which English description identifies a passive participle that is outside this admitted pair?
Написанное письмо лежит на столе.
Admitted reading: a passive participle is outside this bookish pair
Which English description identifies text that is merely on paper?
a sentence that is only on paper
Admitted reading: appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Which English description states the limit of this admitted pair?
an adverbial in a different sentence
this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish
Admitted reading: this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish
every adverbial participle is bookish in every sentence
Rejected reading: every adverbial participle is bookish in every sentence
Закончив письмо,
Закончив письмо
Анна пошла на почту.
Анна пошла на почту
Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту
Написанное письмо лежит на столе
shippedText=Admitted reading: appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair

originalSourceText=In this admitted pair, the adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination is the ordinary option.
A passive participle, text that is merely on paper, or a different sentence does not extend this pair.
This pair is not a universal stylistic rule.
Return to the adverbial opening versus the finite coordination in this pair.
Закончив письмо, Анна пошла на почту.
the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту.
the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair
Which English description identifies the opening word of the bookish sentence in this pair?
Закончив
the adverbial word opens the bookish sentence

a passive participle is outside this bookish pair

appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Rejected reading: appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Which English description identifies the ordinary option in this admitted pair?
Admitted reading: the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair
Rejected reading: the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
Which English description identifies a passive participle that is outside this admitted pair?
Написанное письмо лежит на столе.

Which English description identifies text that is merely on paper?
a sentence that is only on paper
Admitted reading: appearance in writing is outside this bookish pair
Which English description states the limit of this admitted pair?
an adverbial in a different sentence
this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish
Admitted reading: this pair does not license every adverbial as bookish
every adverbial participle is bookish in every sentence
Rejected reading: every adverbial participle is bookish in every sentence
Закончив письмо,
Закончив письмо
Анна пошла на почту.
Анна пошла на почту

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:The active past participle is used in order to indicate actions that happened in
originalSourceText=The active past participle is used in order to indicate actions that happened in the past:
:Де́вушка, '''чита́вшая''' тут кни́гу, забы́ла свой телефо́н – The girl '''that read''' this book here forgot her phone (the girl read the book '''in the past''').

Compare:
:Де́вушка, '''чита́ющая''' тут кни́гу, – моя́ сестра́ – The girl '''reading''' this book here is my sister (she is reading the book now, '''in the present''').
shippedText=The active past participle is used in order to indicate actions that happened in the past:
transformation=selection

licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/

shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Russian_grammar&oldid=1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:In standard Russian, adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish s
originalSourceText=In standard Russian, adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish speech; in colloquial language they are usually replaced with single adjectival participles or constructions with verbs: Пообедав, я пошёл гулять ("Having eaten, I went for a walk") → Я пообедал и пошёл гулять ("I ate and went for a walk").
shippedText=adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish speech
transformation=selection
licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0

originalSourceText=In standard Russian, adverbial participles are considered a feature of bookish speech; in colloquial language they are usually replaced with single adjectival participles or constructions with verbs: Пообедав, я пошёл гулять ("Having eaten, I went for a walk") → Я пообедал и пошёл гулять ("I ate and went for a walk").

recordType=instruction
sourceUrl=https://ru.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Причастие_(лингвистика)&oldid=153078996
sourceRevision=153078996
sourceRecordId=prichastie:В русском языке причастный оборот часто выделяется [[Запятая|запятыми]]. Если пр
originalSourceText=В русском языке причастный оборот часто выделяется [[Запятая|запятыми]]. Если причастный оборот стоит после определяемого слова, он выделяется запятыми с двух сторон. Когда причастный оборот стоит перед определяемым словом, запятые не ставятся, кроме тех случаев, когда определяемое слово выражено личным местоимением или причастный оборот обозначает причину. Если после причастного оборота конец предложения, то запятая ставится только перед причастным оборотом.

'''Примеры:'''
* ''Программа, написанная в спешке, выполнила недопустимую операцию.''
* ''Написанная в спешке программа выполнила недопустимую операцию.
shippedText=Когда причастный оборот стоит перед определяемым словом, запятые не ставятся, кроме тех случаев, когда определяемое слово выражено личным местоимением или причастный оборот обозначает причину.

licenseId=CC BY-SA 4.0
licenseUrl=https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/

shareAlikeTreatment=Adapted curriculum data, selection, and arrangement are distributed under CC BY-SA 4.0. Application code, user data, and user recordings are outside that statement. No audio is relicensed because this unit ships no audio.

originalSourceText=such as {{wikt-lang|ru|беж}} ('beige', non-adapted form of {{wikt-lang|ru|бе́жевый}}) or {{lang|ru|ха́ки}} ('khaki-colored'), most adjectives follow one of a small number of regular declension patterns (except for some that complicate the {{wikt-lang|en|short form}}). sourceId=src-wikipedia-russian-grammar-1369617627
sourceRevision=1369617627
sourceRecordId=grammar:The active past participle is used in order to indicate actions that happened in
sourceHash=dee66aeeccb1cff104490a5dd0378a8f9f44bf6570a07d2fa906224785b1e9b8
supportedClaim=active-participle
exactSpan=The active past participle is used in order to indicate actions that happened in the past:
:Де́вушка, '''чита́вшая''' тут кни́гу, забы́ла свой телефо́н – The girl '''that read''' this book here forgot her phone (the girl read the book '''in the past''').

Compare:
:Де́вушка, '''чита́ющая''' тут кни́гу, – моя́ сестра́ – The girl '''reading''' this book here is my sister (she is reading the book now, '''in the present''').

sourceRecordId=prichastie:В русском языке причастный оборот часто выделяется [[Запятая|запятыми]]. Если пр
sourceHash=821d77bb38feb19b79078e8af3f507f2fcaa4d7e6735f4c1d10a2475a73cbc9a
supportedClaim=participle-punctuation
exactSpan=В русском языке причастный оборот часто выделяется [[Запятая|запятыми]]. Если причастный оборот стоит после определяемого слова, он выделяется запятыми с двух сторон. Когда причастный оборот стоит перед определяемым словом, запятые не ставятся, кроме тех случаев, когда определяемое слово выражено личным местоимением или причастный оборот обозначает причину. Если после причастного оборота конец предложения, то запятая ставится только перед причастным оборотом.

'''Примеры:'''
* ''Программа, написанная в спешке, выполнила недопустимую операцию.''
* ''Написанная в спешке программа выполнила недопустимую операцию.

exactSpan=Read the passage for the actions, not for whether the style is bookish.

she went to the post office after finishing
отправила его
she sent the letter from the post office

Анна
Anna is the person who did the actions

the letter went to the post office alone
the post office wrote the letter

Which English reading matches what happened before Anna left?
Закончив письмо
she finished the letter before leaving
Admitted reading: she finished the letter before leaving
she sent the letter before writing it
she left before the letter existed
Rejected reading: she sent the letter before writing it
Rejected reading: she left before the letter existed
Which English reading matches where Anna went after finishing?
пошла на почту

she stayed at the table after finishing
she finished the letter at the market

she left the letter at home
she received a letter at home

exactSpan=In this admitted pair, the adverbial opening is the bookish option and the finite coordination is the ordinary option.
A passive participle, text that is merely on paper, or a different sentence does not extend this pair.
This pair is not a universal stylistic rule.
Return to the adverbial opening versus the finite coordination in this pair.

the adverbial opening is the bookish option in this pair
Анна закончила письмо и пошла на почту.
the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair

Закончив
the adverbial word opens the bookish sentence

a passive participle is outside this bookish pair

Admitted reading: the finite coordination is the ordinary option in this pair

sourceHash=0b5b1bad657a8c901ba9f471ff7adff3a945be6f281aa760f58c59fd0f8c78e8

exactSpan=such as {{wikt-lang|ru|беж}} ('beige', non-adapted form of {{wikt-lang|ru|бе́жевый}}) or {{lang|ru|ха́ки}} ('khaki-colored'), most adjectives follow one of a small number of regular declension patterns (except for some that complicate the {{wikt-lang|en|short form}}). In modern Russian, the short form appears only in the nominative and is used when the adjective is in a [[predicate (grammar)|predicative]] role: {{lang|ru|нов, нова́, но́во, новы́}} are short forms of {{lang|ru|но́вый}} ('new').
